***Vareṇyam*** **means the best among all lights, the supreme abode or that which is always chosen by persons desiring material or spiritual benefit.**

*vṛṇoter varaṇārthatvāt jāgrat-svapnādi-varjitam*  
*nityaṁ śuddhaṁ buddham ekaṁ nityaṁ bhargam adhīśvaram*

**This is because the verb** ***vṛn*** **means “to choose.” There is one supreme eternal light, the Lord, who is pure intelligence, devoid of waking, sleep and dreaming.**

*ahaṁ brahma paraṁ jyotir dhyāyema hi vimuktaye*  
*taj jyotir bhagavān viṣṇur jagaj-janmādi-kāraṇam*

**We should meditate on that supreme light known as “I” for liberation. That light is Lord Viṣṇu, the cause of the creation and destruction of the universe.**

*śivaṁ kecit paṭhanti sma śakti-rūpaṁ paṭhanti ca*  
*kecit sūryaṁ kecid agniṁ daivatāny agni-hotriṇaḥ*

**Some claim that this light refers to Śiva. Others say it is śakti. Some say it is the sun and some say it is Agni. The performers of sacrifice say it is all the devatās.**

*agny-ādi-rūpī viṣṇur hi vedādau brahma gīyate*  
*tat padaṁ paramaṁ viṣṇor devasya savituḥ smṛtam*

**Viṣṇu endowed with the form of Agni is glorified as Brahman in the Vedas. Brahman or light is also called the supreme abode of Viṣṇu, who is also known as deva and savitṛ.**

*dadhāter vā dhīmahīti manasā dhārayemahi*  
*no 'smākaṁ yac ca bhargas tat sarveṣāṁ prāṇināṁ dhiyaḥ*  
*codayāt prerayād buddhiṁ bhoktṛnāṁ sarva-karmasu*  
*dṛṣṭādṛṣṭa-vipākeṣu viṣṇuḥ sūryāgni-rūpa-bhāk*

**Thus the meaning is “We should hold in the mind (dhīmahi) that supreme (vareṇyam) abode (bhargo) of Viṣṇu (savituḥ devasya).” May bhargas, the Lord, (yaḥ), inspire the intelligence of all living beings (naḥ dhiyaḥ pracodayāt). May Viṣṇu, who also has the forms of the sun and Agni, inspire the intelligence of all enjoyers of their karmas both visible and invisible.**

*īśvara-prerito gacchet svargaṁ vā śvabhram eva vā*  
*īśāvasyam idaṁ sarvaṁ mahad-ādi-jagad dhariḥ*

**Directed by the Lord, the jīva will go to heaven or hell. This whole universe, made of mahat-tattva and other elements, is pervaded by the Lord.**

*svargādyaiḥ krīḍate devo yo haṁsaḥ puruṣaḥ prabhuḥ*  
*dhyānena puruṣo 'yaṁ ca draṣṭavyaḥ sūrya-maṇḍale*

**The Lord who is known as deva, Haṁsa, puruṣa and prabhu, who plays in Svarga and other places, should be seen in the sun by meditation.**

*satyaṁ sadāśivaṁ brahma viṣṇor yat paramaṁ padam*  
*devasya savitur devo vareṇyaṁ hi turīyakam*

**The supreme abode of Lord Viṣṇu or Savit, which is also called Satyam, Sadāśiva and Brahman, and is most excellent (vareṇyam), being the fourth place, the spiritual place.**

*yo 'sāv āditya-puruṣaḥ so sāv aham anuttamam*  
*janānāṁ śubha-karmādīn pravārtayati yaḥ sadā*

**He who is the person in the sun and who always inspires the pious acts of humanity is the incomparable self (aham).**

*yatrādhikṛtya gāyatrīṁ varṇyate dharma-vistaraḥ*  
*vṛtrāsura-vadhotsiktaṁ tad bhāgavatam ucyate*

**That Purāṇa beginning with gāyatrī in which dharma and the killing of Vṛtrāsura are described is called Bhāgavatam.**

The words *bhargas*, Brahman, *para*, Viṣṇu, all mean Bhagavān. Wherever they are used, they mean Bhagavān. Where worship with identity is indicated (*ahamgrahopāsanā,* mentioned above with *so sāv aham anuttamam*), it indicates only similarity (not complete identity). By that the person is qualified for worshipping the Lord.

The meaning of the ten characteristics of the Purāṇa is seen in the Bhāgavatam verse also. *Sarga, visarga, sthāna, nirodha* are indicated by *janmādy asya yataḥ.* *Manvantara* and īśānukathā, which occur during maintenance or *sthāna,* are included in it. *Poṣana* is indicated by *tene brahma hrḍā*. *Ūti* is indicated by *muhyanti.* Mukti of the jīvas which occurs when the jīva approaches the Lord is indicated by his śakti (*dhāmnā*) which destroys all illusions *(nirasta-kuhaka*). The *āśraya* is indicated by *satyam param.*

It is clear from the previous discussion that Kṛṣṇa alone is indicated, since he is discerned to have the position of Svayam Bhagavān. Thus, from the first verse of Bhāgavatam, which is the initial statement or upakrama, it is clear that the form of Bhagavān, who has form and qualities, is the object of meditation in all verses and statements of the work. That is a suitable conclusion since it is also made clear from other statements describing his svarūpa.

*yo ’syotprekṣaka ādi-madhya-nidhane yo ’vyakta-jīveśvaro*  
*yaḥ sṛṣṭvedam anupraviśya ṛṣiṇā cakre puraḥ śāsti tāḥ*  
*yaṁ sampadya jahāty ajām anuśayī suptaḥ kulāyaṁ yathā*  
*taṁ kaivalya-nirasta-yonim abhayaṁ dhyāyed ajasraṁ harim*

**He is the Lord who eternally watches over this universe, before, during and after its manifestation. He is the master of both the unmanifest material energy and the spirit soul. After sending forth the creation he enters within it, accompanying each living entity. There he creates the material bodies and then remains as their regulator. By surrendering to him the** ***jīva*** **covered with** ***upādhis*** **can escape the embrace of illusion, just as a dreaming person forgets his own body. One who wants liberation from fear should constantly meditate upon this Lord, who destroys** ***māyā*** **by his pure** ***svarūpa*** **and gives freedom from fear. SB 10.97.50**

The same conclusion may be reached by many other statements such as dharmaḥ projjhita and kim vā parair:

*dharmaḥ projjhita-kaitavo ’tra paramo nirmatsarāṇāṁ satāṁ*  
*vedyaṁ vāstavam atra vastu śivadaṁ tāpa-trayonmūlanam*  
*śrīmad-bhāgavate mahā-muni-kṛte kiṁ vā parair īśvaraḥ*  
*sadyo hṛdy avarudhyate ’tra kṛtibhiḥ śuśrūṣubhis tat-kṣaṇāt \|\|*

**The Supreme Lord becomes immediately captured in the heart of the accomplished devotees by hearing** ***Bhāgavatam*** **and even by those who have suddenly developed a desire to hear it. This does not happened with other works. In the** ***Bhāgavatam*** **alone, created by the Lord himself, is presented the real, permanent object which can be understood by those without selfish intentions, and which bestows auspiciousness and release from the material world of miseries. In the** ***Bhāgavatam*** **alone is presented the process for attaining that highest object, devoid of all material goals and liberation. SB 1.1.2**

The verse is spoken by Vyāsa.

## 106.

The upasaṁhāra or concluding statement should not stray far from the upakrama or introductory statement.

*kasmai yena vibhāsito 'yam atulo jñāna-pradīpaḥ purā*  
*tad-rūpeṇa ca nāradāya munaye kṛṣṇāya tad-rūpiṇā*  
*yogīndrāya tad-ātmanātha bhagavad-rātāya kāruṇyatas*  
*tac chuddhaṁ vimalaṁ viśokam amṛtaṁ satyaṁ paraṁ dhīmahi*

**I meditate upon that pure, spotless Lord who is free from suffering and death and who previously revealed this incomparable lamp of knowledge to Brahmā. Brahmā then spoke it to the sage Nārada, who narrated it to Kṛṣṇa-dvaipāyana Vyāsa. Śrīla Vyāsa revealed this** ***Bhāgavatam*** **to the greatest of sages, Śukadeva Gosvāmī, and Śukadeva mercifully spoke it to Mahārāja Parīkṣit. SB 12.13.19**

Words like *kasmai yena vibhāsito ‘yam* (the Lord who revealed the scripture to Brahmā) correspond to what was described in the introductory verse. In his commentary on *ātmā-gṛhītir itaravad* (Brahma-sūtra 3.3.17) Śaṅkāra explains that the word *sat* in the introductory statement means *ātmā* because of the word *ātmā* in the concluding statement. Similarly, in this concluding verse it is shown that the speaker of the *catuhślokī* (who spoke to Brahmā) is Bhagavān (and not Garbhodakaśāyī Viṣṇu, since there must be correspondence with the introductory verse) and that Bhagavān alone is the object of Vyāsa’s meditation. The work became most attractive to Śukadeva because of this.

*sva-sukha-nibhṛta-cetās tad-vyudastānya-bhāvo*  
*'py ajita-rucira-līlākṛṣṭa-sāras tadīyam*  
*vyatanuta kṛpayā yas tattva-dīpaṁ purāṇaṁ*  
*tam akhila-vṛjina-ghnaṁ vyāsa-sūnuṁ nato 'smi*

**Let me offer my respectful obeisances unto the son of Vyāsadeva, Śukadeva Gosvāmī, who destroys of all sin, and who, though fixed in his own bliss with no distractions, became attracted to the pastimes of Kṛṣṇa and mercifully spread the** ***Bhāgavatam*** **which describes Kṛṣṇa and reveals the** ***rasa*** **of his pastimes. SB 12.12.69**

The meaning of the *upasaṁhāra* verse is this. This work was revealed (*vibhasitaḥ)* to Brahmā, situated in the lotus in the navel of Garbhodakaśāyī, by Bhagavān, whose form is described in the Second Canto, and who showed Mahā-vaikuṇṭha to Brahmā. The work was not produced at that time however. The Bhāgavatam (*ayam*) at the beginning of first parārdha (*purā*) was given by Brahmā (*tad-rūpena*) to Nārada and later by Nārada (*tad-rūpinā*) to Vyāsa (*kṛṣṇāya*). And by Vyāsa (*tad-ātmanā*) it was given to Śukadeva (*yogīndrāya*). It was given by Śukadeva (*tad-ātmanā,* understood) to Parīkṣit (*bhagavad-rātāya*). By using the phrases *tad-rūpeṇa, tad-rūpinā,*  and*tad-ātmanā* it is indicated that not only *was catuḥślokī* revealed each time but that, absorbed in their forms (*tad-rūpeṇa* etc.), they revealed the complete Purāṇa. It should be understood that finally Sūta speaks to the sages and says “I also speak the Purāṇa to you.” However he does not utter this out of humility. Thus the greatness of the teachers of Bhāgavatam among all teachers is shown. The *sampradāya* through Saṅkarṣaṇa is not separately mentioned as it is included in the revelation of Vyāsa. SB 3.8.7 relates that Saṅkarṣaṇa spoke the Bhāgavatam to Sanat-kumāra.3

We should meditate on the supreme being known as Bhagavān (*param satyam dhīmahi*). *Param* means Bhagavān as in the *sahasra nāma stotra. Yat tat param anutttamam:* he is the supreme Bhagavān without compare. *Ādyo ‘vatāraḥ puruṣah parasya*: the first avatāra (Mahāviṣṇu) is an expansion of Bhagavān (*parasya*). (SB 2.6.42)

This verse also explains that the work is the meaning of gāyatrī, shown by the word *dhīmahi,* since it stimulates the intelligence of Brahmā and others. It is said *gāyatrī-bhāṣya-rūpo’ sau bhāratārtha-vinirṇayaḥ:*  Bhāgavatam is the commentary on the gāyatrī and explains the Mahābhārata. (Garuḍa Purāṇa) Sūta speaks the above verse.

### 107

*Abhyāsa* (repetition of the theme, as well as *apūrvatā,* unique qualities*)*  is represented by the following:

*kali-mala-saṁhati-kālano 'khileśo*  
*harir itaratra na gīyate hy abhīkṣṇam*  
*iha tu punar bhagavān aśeṣa-mūrtiḥ*  
*paripaṭhito 'nu-padaṁ kathā-prasaṅgaiḥ*

**Nārāyaṇa, the soul of the universal form, who annihilates the accumulated sins of the Kali age, is not glorified much in other works. But Bhagavān, who has unlimited forms, is abundantly and constantly described throughout the various narrations of this** ***Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*****. SB 12.12.66**

Nārāyaṇa, who is the antaryāmī of the universe (*akhileśaḥ*) or Viṣṇu, its protector, who is the destroyer (*kālanaḥ)* of the collection of sins of Kali, is not glorified or is not glorified much (*abhīkṣṇam)* in other works (*itaratra*) which propound karma or Brahman. The word *tu* indicates emphasis. But Bhagavān is glorified constantly in Bhāgavatam (*iha*). He who has unlimited avatāra forms (*aśeṣa-mūrtiḥ*) is glorified as such. In other works he is described without discernment. Described in each verse with various narrations, Bhagavān alone is revealed (*paṭhitah)* completely (*pari*). This verse also indicates *apūrvatā* (uniqueness) of the Bhāgavatam, since this form cannot be understood through other works. Sūta speaks the verse.

## 108.

The phala (resul) is described:

*pibanti ye bhagavata ātmanaḥ satāṁ*  
*kathāmṛtaṁ śravaṇa-puṭeṣu sambhṛtam \|*  
*punanti te viṣaya-vidūṣitāśayaṁ*  
*vrajanti tac-caraṇa-saroruhāntikam \|\|*

**Those who drink the sweet pastimes of the Lord and his devotees held in the cups of their ears clean their hearts of all contamination and attain the lotus feet of the Lord for service. SB 2.2.37**

Those who drink the sweet pastimes of the Lord who is the life *(ātmanaḥ*) of the devotees (*satām*) clean their hearts. Or those who drink the sweet pastimes of the Lord whom the devotees take as their own *(ātmanaḥ*) clean their hearts. They have possesssiveness, taking him as their possession. The chief sweet stories are in the Bhāgavatam, the object of study.

*yasyāṁ vai śrūyamāṇāyāṁ kṛṣṇe parama-pūruṣe \|*  
*bhaktir utpadyate puṁsaḥ śoka-moha-bhayāpahā \|\|*

**By hearing** ***Bhāgavatam, bhakti*** **for the Supreme Lord Kṛṣṇa appears. Hearing it also destroys lamentation, illusion and fear in the human being. SB 1.1.7**

Śukadeva speaks the verse.

## 109.

*Arthavada* (praise) is expressed in the following:

*yaṁ brahmā varuṇendra-rudra-marutaḥ stunvanti divyaiḥ stavair*  
*vedaiḥ sāṅga-pada-kramopaniṣadair gāyanti yaṁ sāma-gāḥ*  
*dhyānāvasthita-tad-gatena manasā paśyanti yaṁ yogino*  
*yasyāntaṁ na viduḥ surāsura-gaṇā devāya tasmai namaḥ*

**Unto that personality whom Brahmā, Varuṇa, Indra, Rudra and the Maruts praise by chanting transcendental hymns of the Vedas with all their corollaries,** ***pada-kramas*** **and Upaniṣads, whom the chanters of the Sāma Veda glorify, whom the perfected** ***yogīs*** **see within their minds after fixing themselves in meditation, and whom neither the** ***devatās*** **or demons can fathom—unto Bhagavān I offer my respects. SB 12.12.1**

Brahmā and others praise Bhagavān with verses of praise (stavaiḥ) and the Vedas. The yogīs see him by their minds absorbed in him by being fixed through meditation (dhyānāvastita-tad-gatena). Sūta speaks the verse.

## 110.

Upapatti (reasoning) is expressed in the following:

*bhagavān sarva-bhūteṣu lakṣitaḥ svātmanā hariḥ \|*  
*dṛśyair buddhy-ādibhir draṣṭā lakṣaṇair anumāpakaiḥ \|\|*

**The devotee understands the Supreme Lord in all beings by the presence of Paramātmā.** **The** ***jīva*** **is inferred by symptoms such as their observable intelligence and by conclusive inference. SB 2.2.35**

First, the jīva *(draṣṭā*) is known (*lakṣitaḥ)* by intelligence and other means (*buddhy-ādibhiḥ*). Two ways are shown. The jīva is known by inconclusive reasoning (*lakṣaṇaiḥ*) concerning a conscious seer: perception (*dṛśyaiḥ*) of insentient objects like intelligence cannot take place without a seer who is self-revealing or conscious. The jīva is also known by conclusive reasoning or *vyāpti (anumāpakaiḥ*) Vyāpti is universal concomitance: a rule without an exception. 4: intelligence must be employed by an agent, just as an axe needs a person holding it in order to cut a tree.

Bhagavān is also known. How? By antaryāmī (svātmanā), his aṁśa, who has entered into all beings who are the seers (sarva-bhūteṣu). First, antaryāmī is known by all seers. Then by that knowledge, Bhagavān is understood. That is inferred in two ways as previously. Antaryāmī is perceived by preliminary logic: the action of all the jīvas cannot occur without a particular inner instigator, since one sees that the doer and enjoyer (jīva) is not independent and since karma as a cause of his actions is insentient.

*eṣa hy anenātmanā cakṣuṣā darśayati. srotreṇa śrāvayati. manasā mānayati. buddhyā bodhayati. tasmād etāv āhuḥ sṛtir asṛtiḥ*

**The Paramātmā makes the jīva see by the eye, hear by the ear, contemplate by the mind, become aware by the intelligence. Therefore they call one the known (jīva) and the other the unknown (Lord). Bhāllaveya-śruti**

Next, Bhagavān is provisionally inferred by his aṁśa, antaryāmī, since antaryāmī cannot be concluded as the final being by the following logic: if the Lord enters with all his aṁśas into the jīvas (instead of just a portion of his whole self) for showing his power as antaryāmī, then he could not be the Lord, since he would no longer be complete. Gītā says:

*atha vā bahunaitena kiṁ jñātena tavārjuna \|*  
*viṣṭabhyāham idaṁ kṛtsnam ekāṁśena sthito jagat \|\|*

**But what is the use of knowing all these individual** ***vibhūtis*****, Arjuna? Please understand: by just one portion of Myself, I am firmly established in this universe. BG 10.42**

Viṣnu Purāṇa says *sva-śakti-leśāvrta-bhūta-sargaḥ*: all beings and creations are pervaded by a particle of the Lord’s śakti.

It was concluded that the jīva is an entity who is impelled by a doer acting as the instigator since he is not independent. He is like a carpenter. In this way, just as antaryāmī has been proved by invariable concomitance (*vyāpti*), so Bhagavān is also proved by the same means: antaryāmī who dwells in the jīva of insignificant power is īśvara, but antaryāmī takes shelter of Bhagavān who is his shelter and source, just as the power of the carpenter to act takes shelter of the power of the king.

The following is also said in Bhāgavatam:

*yathendriyaiḥ pṛthag-dvārair artho bahu-guṇāśrayaḥ*  
*eko nāneyate tadvad bhagavān śāstra-vartmabhiḥ*

**Just as one object, the substrate of many qualities, is perceived by the various senses in various ways, so Bhagavān is perceived variously by the various scriptural practices. SB 3.32.33**

By this, it is proved that the goal of all scriptures is the same (Bhagavān). Śukadeva speaks the verse. In this way the verse *vadanti tat tattva-vidah* is supported.

Here ends Paramātmā Sandarbha, the third among the Sandarbhas which use Bhāgavatam as their source, and which contains the expert instructions of Rūpa and Sanātana Gosvāmīs, most revered members of the grand assembly of all Vaiṣṇavas, following the lotus feet of Lord Kṛṣṇa Caitanya-deva, who descended to distribute his worship, in order to purify Kali-yuga.

The Paramātma Sandarbha is the third of the Sandarbhas, which are the essence of all collections of verses.

Anupatti vyāpti.??/ bṛ.ā.u. 2.4.10\]
